MERML.Assortment.plan_to_carry_qtyDECIMAL(18,4)Target stocking level · drives replenishment service (RC-E2E-05)

9.3 EHRML — Electronic Health Records

EHRML is HLS-only. The schema is shaped to FHIR-equivalent semantics but stored as canonical APEX columns for joinability.

DDL — EHRML.Encounter · EHRML.Diagnosis (sketches)
CREATE TABLE EHRML.Encounter (
    encounter_id        VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    member_id           VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,    -- tokenized — see §7
    provider_id         VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    facility_id         VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    encounter_type      VARCHAR(16)  NOT NULL,    -- 'inpatient' | 'outpatient' | 'ed' | 'tele'
    admission_ts        TIMESTAMP,
    discharge_ts        TIMESTAMP,
    chief_complaint_code VARCHAR(16),             -- ICD-10 or SNOMED code
    drg_code            VARCHAR(8),
    los_hours           AS (DATEDIFF(HOUR, admission_ts, discharge_ts)),
    event_ts            TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    ingest_ts           TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    silver_ts           TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    src_system          VARCHAR(32)  NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (encounter_id)
);

CREATE TABLE EHRML.Diagnosis (
    diagnosis_id        VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    encounter_id        VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL REFERENCES EHRML.Encounter(encounter_id),
    diagnosis_code      VARCHAR(16)  NOT NULL,   -- ICD-10
    diagnosis_type      VARCHAR(16)  NOT NULL,   -- 'principal' | 'secondary' | 'admit'
    seq                 INT          NOT NULL,
    poa_indicator       CHAR(1),                  -- present-on-admission
    event_ts            TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    silver_ts           TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (diagnosis_id)
);

member_id is tokenized at ingest (§6.2) and the unwrap key sits behind the dual-control HSM access policy (§7.3). The Silver layer never holds raw PHI; the unwrap happens at HITL approval moment for a single record.

9.4 Schema-Evolution Rules

The schema library is a living thing. New Services drive new column requirements; new client SOR variants drive new conformance edge cases. Three rules keep evolution from breaking the library:

  1. Additive only by default. Adding columns is free; renaming columns is a versioned migration. SCML v2 introducing on_consignment_qty next to on_hand_qty is fine; renaming available_qty would be a breaking change requiring a coordinated Service migration.
  2. Practice-specific extensions, not forks. If RC needs a column that ER doesn't, add the column to the shared SCML schema with a nullable default; don't fork SCML into SCML-RC and SCML-ER. The library is one library.
  3. Source-system specifics in src_* columns. If SAP carries an extra field that other ERPs don't, it lands in src_sap_*. Canonical APEX consumers don't read it; SAP-specific Bronze→Silver edge cases use it.

9.5 Cross-Schema Composition for RC-E2E-03 — A Worked View

Individual schema ERDs are the dictionary. The actual data graph a Service traverses cuts across schemas. RC-E2E-03 reads SCML (inventory + lot), MERML (markdown · elasticity · competitor), PROML (pricing · discount rules), composes them through Silver into Gold marts, and exposes those marts to agents via MCP. The diagram below collapses that whole pipeline onto one canvas so an architect can see the joins and the materialization edges in a single view.

9.5.1 Cross-Schema Field Map

One last table — the column-by-column map of which Silver columns flow into which Gold mart. This is the practical materialization spec a data engineer reads when building or auditing the Gold-build notebook.

Gold mart · columnSilver source · columnTransformation
g_excursion_decision_panel.lot_idSCML.Lot.lot_iddirect (cross-Service)
g_excursion_decision_panel.fsma204_statusSCML.Lot.fsma204_statusdirect
g_excursion_decision_panel.at_risk_qtySCML.Inventory.on_hand_qtyfilter on lot_id, sum
g_excursion_decision_panel.recent_markdownsMERML.Markdownlast 3 by sku_id, location_id, JSON-array
g_pricing_recommendation_basis.elasticity_coefMERML.Elasticity.elasticity_coeffilter on max(estimated_at) for window

Best Practice — Use this view as the materialization spec

The cross-schema field map is what the Microsoft Fabric notebook author refers to when building gold-materialize-pricing-recommendation-basis.ipynb. The spec is also what Microsoft Purview registers as lineage edges when the notebook runs — every Gold-column → Silver-column edge becomes a Purview lineage record. Keep this table in sync with the notebook; if they diverge, lineage drifts and audits get harder.

Key Takeaways

  • SCML, MERML, EHRML (and the rest from Ch 2) are concrete column-level entities — DDL-grade, not conceptual.
  • Every Silver row carries three timestamps (event/ingest/silver), source-system lineage columns (src_*), and where appropriate a LEDGER reference (decision_ledger_id) that closes the audit loop.
  • Schema evolution is additive by default; practice-specific needs add nullable columns, never fork the schema; source-system quirks live in src_* columns.
  • The cross-schema view (§9.5) is the practical materialization spec; keep it synced with the Microsoft Fabric Gold-build notebooks so Microsoft Purview lineage stays accurate.
Chapter 10

Quality, Lineage, Microsoft Purview

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the Microsoft Purview integration that catalogs every Bronze → Silver → Gold artifact.
  • Establishes the Silver-tier data-quality contract: what gets checked, what blocks promotion to Gold, what alerts.
  • Maps lineage to the audit chain so the LEDGER decision links back to the Bronze rows that supported it.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Microsoft Purview catalogs every Bronze table · Silver entity · Gold mart · MCP tool · LEDGER asset, with lineage edges between them.
  • Sensitivity labels mirror the §7.1 PII tier classification and propagate through lineage automatically.
  • Six classes of quality check gate Silver→Gold promotion (schema · referential · volume · distribution · recency · cross-tier).
  • The LEDGER → Gold → Silver → Bronze → SOR lineage chain is the audit-grade path a regulator can walk.

10.1 Microsoft Purview as the Catalog and Lineage Surface

Microsoft Purview is the catalog. Every Bronze table, every Silver entity, every Gold mart, every MCP tool, every sor-connections.yaml entry is registered there. Microsoft Purview consumes:

  • Microsoft Fabric Lakehouse and Warehouse asset catalogs (auto-scan).
  • Microsoft Fabric KQL DB and Eventstream registrations.
  • Microsoft Fabric Pipeline and Notebook lineage events.
  • The MCP tool catalog (registered via a custom Purview type emitted by the MCP host on startup).
  • The LEDGER write events (registered as a custom Purview asset type).

The result is a single catalog where a service architect can ask "which agents read which Gold marts that derive from which Silver entities that derive from which Bronze tables that come from which client SOR systems?" and get an answer rather than a series of joins across documentation.

10.2 Sensitivity Labels and Classifications

Microsoft Purview sensitivity labels mirror the four-tier PII classification from §7.1. Once Bronze is ingested with a tier-3 or tier-4 field, the sensitivity label propagates automatically through Silver and Gold lineage. An analyst opening the Gold mart in Power BI sees the label; an MCP tool definition that returns a tier-3 column gets flagged.

Auto-classification rules cover obvious patterns (email addresses, SSN-shaped strings, NDC codes) and can run on Bronze samples as part of the ingest pipeline. Manual classification overrides the auto-classification when the data shape doesn't match the regex but the field is sensitive (e.g., a free-text "notes" column that operationally contains member-identifiable information).

10.3 The Silver Quality Contract

Silver promotion to Gold is gated by quality checks. The checks are run by a Microsoft Fabric Notebook activity at the end of each Silver build, before any Gold mart that depends on the entity is rebuilt.

Check classExampleAction on fail
SchemaRequired columns present, types match libraryBlock Gold rebuild · alert on-call
ReferentialEvery EHRML.Diagnosis.encounter_id resolves to a row in EHRML.EncounterBlock Gold rebuild · alert on-call
VolumeSilver row count within 3σ of seven-day moving averageWarn · proceed with Gold rebuild
DistributionMedian on_hand_qty for top SKUs within ±20% of trailing windowWarn · proceed
RecencyNewest event_ts within expected lag for source cadenceBlock Gold rebuild · alert on-call
Cross-tierSilver row count == sum of Bronze rows it derives from (post-dedup)Block · open data-quality ticket

The check results are themselves Purview events; over time a quality timeline accrues per Silver entity. When an architect investigates a Service KPI miss, the quality timeline is the first place to look — KPI deviations correlated to upstream quality alerts is a much faster diagnosis than KPI deviations as a free-floating mystery.

10.4 Lineage to LEDGER

The audit chain in the Deployment Guide (§6.3.1, Ch 5) records every agent decision with a row hash and a backing MCP tool reference. The MCP tool reference points at a Gold mart. The Gold mart's lineage in Purview points at Silver entities. The Silver entities' lineage points at Bronze tables. The Bronze tables' lineage points at the SOR connection.

For any LEDGER row, a six-hop reconstruction reaches back to the source-of-record system and the timestamp at which it produced the supporting fact. That is the audit-grade chain a regulator can walk: "on date X, this Service decided Y; the agent that decided it called this MCP tool; the MCP tool returned this row hash from this Gold mart; the Gold mart was materialized from these Silver entities; the Silver entities were built from these Bronze rows; the Bronze rows came from these SOR systems via this connection at this timestamp."

Independence — Microsoft Purview is the audit-narrative substrate

Microsoft Purview running in the client tenant is what makes the two-contract model defensible to a client's audit committee. The Deloitte authoring artifacts that crossed the Independence boundary (agent images, Bicep, schema definitions) are visible as registered assets; the client SOR systems and client data lineage are visible as the things they connect into. The single picture is what an auditor walks. Without Purview, the picture has to be reconstructed from a half-dozen sources.

Key Takeaways

  • Microsoft Purview is the unified catalog: every Bronze table, Silver entity, Gold mart, MCP tool, and LEDGER row is a registered asset, with lineage edges between them.
  • Sensitivity labels mirror the PII tier classification and propagate through lineage automatically.
  • Silver-to-Gold promotion is gated by six classes of quality check; failures block Gold rebuild and alert on-call.
  • The lineage graph from LEDGER → Gold → Silver → Bronze → SOR is the audit-grade chain a regulator walks.
Part IV

Gold Tier — Decision-Ready Data

Gold is where the question shape lives. Bronze preserves what the SOR said; Silver conforms what the SOR said; Gold answers what the agent asks. Every Gold mart is a denormalized, latency-tuned, agent-queryable answer-shape — and there is exactly one mart per question across the catalog. This part walks the Microsoft Fabric Lakehouse and Warehouse patterns that hold Gold, the per-Service Gold mart inventory, and the MCP tool surface that exposes those marts to agents.

Chapters in this Part

  • Chapter 11Microsoft Fabric Lakehouse Patterns
  • Chapter 12Per-Service Gold Marts
  • Chapter 13Agent MCP Tool Surface
Chapter 11

Microsoft Fabric Lakehouse Patterns

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the Microsoft Fabric Warehouse + Direct Lake + semantic-model pattern for serving Gold to agents and operators.
  • Establishes the four mart shapes used across the catalog (point-in-time, panel, time-series, attribution).
  • Explains why Gold marts are materialized rather than computed on demand.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Gold lives in Microsoft Fabric Warehouse (T-SQL surface) backed by OneLake Delta; Direct Lake provides zero-copy Power BI on top.
  • Four mart shapes — point-in-time · decision-panel (~40% of marts) · time-series · attribution — cover every agent question across the catalog.
  • Marts are materialized, not on-demand: latency · predictability · cost all favor materialization at APEX volume.
  • Three triggers: scheduled (cron) · upstream-event (Eventstream from Silver) · Direct Lake auto-refresh.

11.1 Where Gold Lives

Gold marts are served from Microsoft Fabric Warehouse (T-SQL surface over the same OneLake storage that hosts the Lakehouse). Direct Lake mode in Power BI lets the same Gold mart power operator dashboards without copying data into a tabular model. Semantic models on top of Gold marts are the surface analysts and HITL approvers see; MCP tools are the surface agents see. Both surfaces query the same materialized marts.

Microsoft Fabric componentResource typeRole in Gold
Microsoft Fabric WarehouseMicrosoft.Fabric/warehousesT-SQL surface · per-Service mart catalog · MCP query target
Microsoft Fabric Lakehouse (Gold zone)Microsoft.Fabric/lakehousesUnderlying Delta Parquet · OneLake storage
Microsoft Fabric Direct Lake semantic modelMicrosoft.Fabric/semanticModelsPower BI surface for operators · zero-copy over Gold
Microsoft Fabric PipelineMicrosoft.Fabric/dataPipelinesMaterialization triggers · scheduled and event-driven
Microsoft Fabric NotebookMicrosoft.Fabric/notebooksMaterialization logic · PySpark or T-SQL per mart

11.2 The Four Mart Shapes

Across the 38-Service catalog, Gold marts fall into four shape categories. The shape is dictated by the question; the question is dictated by the agent's reasoning step.

11.2.1 Point-in-time mart

"Given a key, what is the current state?" One row per key, denormalized, kept fresh by upstream Silver builds.

Examples — g_inventory_position_current (RC-E2E-05) · g_member_eligibility_current (HLS-E2E-04) · g_well_status_current (ER-OG-03) · g_equipment_health_current (ICE-EaaS-04).

11.2.2 Decision-panel mart

"Given a triggering event, what does the agent need to know to decide?" One row per triggering event with all the context joined together.

Examples — g_excursion_decision_panel (RC-E2E-03 cold-chain) · g_prior_auth_decision_panel (HLS-E2E-04) · g_outage_decision_panel (ER-PU-04) · g_irops_passenger_panel (TH-AIR-02).

This is the most common shape across the catalog — roughly 40% of Gold marts. The W2-pilot trigger pattern (event fires → decision panel materializes → agent queries it) is what produces the shape.

11.2.3 Time-series mart

"How has this metric evolved over a rolling window?" Wide rolling-window aggregations partitioned by the dimension the question asks about.

Examples — g_kpi_rc_e2e_03_daily (RC pricing KPI) · g_network_fault_trends_15m (TMT-TEL-NET-02) · g_warranty_intake_weekly (ICE-Aftermarket-03).

11.2.4 Attribution mart

"Of the outcomes observed, which came from agent-suggested actions and which came from baseline?" One row per outcome with the LEDGER decision row that drove it (or null for baseline).

Examples — g_markdown_outcome_attribution (RC-E2E-03) · g_denial_appeal_attribution (HLS-E2E-02) · g_ra_recovery_attribution (TMT-TEL-RAA-04).

Attribution marts are how the Service KPI envelope gets measured. They join LEDGER rows (decisions) with Silver outcomes (the next markdown / appeal / recovery) and produce the gross-margin lift, denial-rate drop, RA-recovery dollars that the Sellers Guide commits to.

11.3 Why Materialized, Not On-Demand

An obvious shortcut: skip materialization, let MCP tools issue ad-hoc T-SQL against Silver. It works for ten requests per minute. It breaks at the volume APEX runs at (RC-E2E-03 alone fires hundreds of agent calls per shift across a 250-store pilot, scaling to thousands at enterprise rollout).

  • Latency. Materialized Gold mart query: ~50–150 ms. Equivalent multi-Silver-table join: 2–8 seconds. The agent's reasoning step needs sub-second context.
  • Predictability. A materialized mart's row hash is stable across reads in the materialization window; an ad-hoc join's result depends on Silver freshness at query time. Audit reproducibility benefits from the former.
  • Cost. Microsoft Fabric Warehouse charges per query; one materialization burst plus N cheap mart reads is much less than N expensive ad-hoc joins.

11.4 Materialization Triggers

Three triggers materialize a Gold mart:

  1. Scheduled — Microsoft Fabric Pipeline runs on cron (most time-series and KPI marts; e.g., g_kpi_rc_e2e_03_daily runs at 02:00 each day).
  2. Upstream-event — Microsoft Fabric Eventstream fires when Silver completes a build; pipeline subscribes (most decision-panel marts; e.g., g_excursion_decision_panel rebuilds when a new excursion fires through Silver).
  3. Direct Lake auto-refresh — Microsoft Fabric Direct Lake observes underlying Delta tables and serves the latest version without an explicit pipeline (most current-state point-in-time marts; the underlying Silver table is the source of truth).

Key Takeaways

  • Gold lives in Microsoft Fabric Warehouse (T-SQL surface) backed by OneLake Delta Parquet; Microsoft Fabric Direct Lake provides zero-copy Power BI on top.
  • Four mart shapes — point-in-time, decision-panel, time-series, attribution — cover every question across the catalog.
  • Marts are materialized, not on-demand: latency, predictability, and cost all favor materialization at APEX volume.
  • Materialization triggers are scheduled (cron), upstream-event (Eventstream from Silver), or Direct Lake auto-refresh.
Chapter 12

Per-Service Gold Marts

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the per-Service mart inventory pattern that Part VI's catalog chapters apply across all 38 Services.
  • Shows the RC-E2E-03 mart inventory in full (one of each shape) as the worked template.
  • Establishes the mart-naming and partitioning conventions that propagate through Appendix C.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Per-Service mart inventory follows a uniform pattern: shape · name · question backed · trigger · KPI contribution.
  • RC-E2E-03 has five marts spanning all four shapes; the inventory shows up identically in every Part VI Service profile.
  • row_hash · silver_max_event_ts · materialized_at are mandatory on every Gold table — they make agent decisions audit-reproducible.

12.1 The Mart Inventory per Service

Every Service profile in Part VI lists its Gold marts using a uniform inventory pattern: shape · name · backs which agent question · materialization trigger · KPI contribution. The pattern is uniform; the contents are Service-specific. Architects onboarding a new Service into a tenant produce this inventory before they write any MCP tool definitions.

12.2 The RC-E2E-03 Worked Template

ShapeNameQuestion backedTriggerKPI contribution
Decision-panelg_excursion_decision_panel"For excursion X, what is the lot, the at-risk inventory, and the recent markdown history?"Eventstream rule (§5.3)Margin protected per event ($142K avg)
Decision-panelg_markdown_proposal_basis"For SKU-store S/L, what is the elasticity, competitor price, current markdown, and demand forecast?"Daily 02:00 + Silver completion eventMarkdown-to-clear ratio +41%

12.2.1 g_excursion_decision_panel — sketch

DDL — g_excursion_decision_panel
CREATE TABLE g_excursion_decision_panel (
    excursion_id        VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    store_id            VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    zone_id             VARCHAR(32)  NOT NULL,
    fired_ts            TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    -- lot context (joined from SCML.Lot)
    lot_id              VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    lot_supplier_id     VARCHAR(64),
    lot_production_date DATE,
    lot_expiration_date DATE,
    lot_fsma204_status  VARCHAR(16),
    -- at-risk inventory (joined from SCML.Inventory)
    at_risk_qty         DECIMAL(18,4),
    at_risk_value_usd   DECIMAL(18,2),
    -- markdown history (joined from MERML.Markdown)
    recent_markdowns    JSONB,        -- last 3 markdowns for this SKU
    -- excursion metrics
    duration_min        INT,
    peak_temp_c         DECIMAL(5,2),
    threshold_c         DECIMAL(5,2),
    -- materialization metadata
    silver_max_event_ts TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    materialized_at     TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    row_hash            VARCHAR(70)  NOT NULL,    -- 'sha256:' + 64 hex
    PRIMARY KEY (excursion_id)
)
PARTITION BY RANGE (fired_ts);

One row per excursion event. All the context the agent needs in a single row. The MCP tool rc_e2e_03.get_excursion_decision_panel from §3.1 reads this mart by (store_id, excursion_id) primary key — sub-100ms.

12.2.2 g_kpi_rc_e2e_03_daily — sketch

DDL — g_kpi_rc_e2e_03_daily
CREATE TABLE g_kpi_rc_e2e_03_daily (
    store_id            VARCHAR(64)  NOT NULL,
    business_date       DATE         NOT NULL,
    -- realized margins
    gross_margin_dollars DECIMAL(18,2),
    gross_margin_pct     DECIMAL(8,4),
    -- inventory health
    days_on_hand_avg     DECIMAL(8,2),
    aged_stock_pct       DECIMAL(8,4),  -- % of inventory > 60 days on hand
    -- markdown effectiveness
    markdowns_proposed_n INT,
    markdowns_approved_n INT,
    markdown_to_clear_ratio DECIMAL(8,4),
    -- excursion outcomes
    excursions_n         INT,
    margin_protected_usd DECIMAL(18,2),
    -- baseline for attribution
    baseline_gm_pct      DECIMAL(8,4), -- 90-day pre-pilot trailing baseline
    delta_gm_pp          AS (gross_margin_pct - baseline_gm_pct) PERSISTED,
    materialized_at      TIMESTAMP    NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (store_id, business_date)
);

This mart is what proves the Sellers Guide commitment. delta_gm_pp rolling up across stores produces the +3.2pp number; days_on_hand_avg rolling up produces the −28%; markdown_to_clear_ratio produces the +41%. Power BI Direct Lake on this mart drives the operator dashboard the Merchandising Director sees daily.

12.3 Naming and Partitioning Conventions

  • Mart name. g_ prefix + question shape. g_excursion_decision_panel is unambiguous; g_inventory is not.
  • Partitioning. Decision-panel marts partition on fired_ts (or equivalent triggering timestamp); time-series marts partition on the period column; point-in-time marts partition on a sensible date. Microsoft Fabric Warehouse partition prune dramatically reduces the cost of high-volume agent queries.
  • Row hash. Every materialized row carries a row_hash column that the MCP tool returns alongside the data. The agent's LEDGER row records the hash; the audit chain links back to the materialized row.
  • Materialization metadata. Every Gold table has silver_max_event_ts (newest Silver row used in the build) and materialized_at (when the build finished). These two columns are how an architect debugs "the agent decided X but the data didn't yet show Y" — the freshness window is right there.

Key Takeaways

  • Per-Service mart inventory follows a uniform pattern: shape · name · question backed · trigger · KPI contribution.
  • RC-E2E-03 has five marts spanning all four shapes; the inventory shows up identically in every Part VI Service profile.
  • row_hash, silver_max_event_ts, and materialized_at are mandatory columns on every Gold table — they make agent decisions audit-reproducible and freshness debug-able.
Chapter 13

Agent MCP Tool Surface

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the MCP host implementation against Microsoft Fabric Warehouse.
  • Shows the read-tool, decision-emit-tool, and HITL-stage-tool patterns that cover every agent interaction in the catalog.
  • Establishes the per-Service tool registration manifest that propagates through Microsoft Purview and the orchestration layer.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Three MCP tool patterns — read (~70%) · decision-emit (~20%) · HITL-stage (~10%) — cover every agent interaction.
  • FastAPI MCP host registers tools at startup, queries Microsoft Fabric Warehouse under mi-apex-merch-mcp, emits LEDGER rows on every call.
  • mcp-manifest.yaml per Service feeds four consumers: the host · Microsoft Purview · the orchestrator · agent prompts.

13.1 Three Tool Patterns

Every MCP tool in the catalog (~165 across all 38 Services) falls into one of three patterns. Knowing the pattern tells you the tool's audit shape, its cost profile, and its security boundary.

13.1.1 Read tool

Reads from a Gold mart. Idempotent. Free of side effects. The most common pattern — about 70% of the catalog.

  • Input: a key (or set of keys) for the Gold mart.
  • Output: the materialized row(s), with row hash.
  • LEDGER: writes a mcp.tool.called row with input args and output hash.
  • Identity: mi-apex-merch-mcp with Microsoft Fabric Warehouse Reader on the per-Service workspace.

13.1.2 Decision-emit tool

Writes a decision row to LEDGER and to a Service-specific decision table (e.g., MERML.Markdown for an approved markdown). About 20% of the catalog.

  • Input: the decision payload + supporting evidence row hashes.
  • Output: the LEDGER row ID + decision row ID + cosign attestation of the LEDGER row.
  • LEDGER: writes a decision.committed row with the full payload.
  • Identity: mi-apex-merch-mcp with Microsoft Fabric Warehouse Writer on the Service's decision tables and append on LEDGER.
  • Side effect: triggers a Microsoft Fabric Eventstream that downstream attribution marts consume.

13.1.3 HITL-stage tool

Stages a HITL approval ask, including PII unlock if needed. About 10% of the catalog.

  • Input: the proposed decision + the human approver's identity + (optional) PII unlock request.
  • Output: a stage ID the orchestrator polls; the actual decision lands when the human approves.
  • LEDGER: writes a hitl.staged row immediately and a hitl.resolved row when the approver responds.
  • Identity: mi-apex-merch-mcp + just-in-time mi-apex-merch-pii-unlock token if a PII unwrap is requested.

13.2 The MCP Host Implementation

The MCP host is a FastAPI app (Python in the MVP, with Java and .NET implementations possible). It runs as a Container App in the Service's resource group, sharing the Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments environment with the agents.

PYTHON — MCP host tool registration (sketch)
from agentic_merch.mcp import MCPHost, ReadTool, DecisionEmitTool, HITLStageTool
from agentic_merch.fabric import FabricWarehouseClient

host = MCPHost(service_code="RC-E2E-03")
warehouse = FabricWarehouseClient(workspace="rc-e2e-03")

# Read tool — backs by g_excursion_decision_panel
host.register(ReadTool(
    name="rc_e2e_03.get_excursion_decision_panel",
    gold_mart="g_excursion_decision_panel",
    input_schema=ExcursionPanelInput,
    output_schema=ExcursionPanelOutput,
    query=lambda args: warehouse.execute(
        "SELECT * FROM g_excursion_decision_panel "
        "WHERE store_id = ? AND excursion_id = ?",
        (args.store_id, args.excursion_id),
    ),
))

# Decision-emit tool — writes MERML.Markdown + LEDGER row
host.register(DecisionEmitTool(
    name="rc_e2e_03.commit_markdown_decision",
    decision_table="MERML.Markdown",
    input_schema=MarkdownDecisionInput,
    output_schema=MarkdownDecisionOutput,
    on_emit=lambda args: warehouse.transactional(
        insert_markdown=args.to_merml_row(),
        ledger_emit={"event": "decision.committed", "service_code": "RC-E2E-03",
                     "agent_id": args.agent_id, "evidence_hashes": args.evidence_hashes},
    ),
))

# HITL stage tool — stages approval, with optional pii_unlock_request
host.register(HITLStageTool(
    name="rc_e2e_03.stage_markdown_approval",
    decision_class="markdown_above_threshold",
    teams_card_template="markdown-adaptive-card.json",
    input_schema=HITLStageInput,
    output_schema=HITLStageOutput,
))

host.run(port=8080)

13.3 The Per-Service MCP Manifest

Every Service ships an mcp-manifest.yaml that enumerates its tools. The manifest is consumed by:

  • The MCP host at startup (registration source of truth).
  • Microsoft Purview (catalog registration).
  • The orchestration layer (n8n / Logic Apps Hybrid workflows reference tool names from this manifest).
  • The agent prompts (the tool descriptions in this manifest are what the LLM sees for tool selection).
YAML — mcp-manifest.yaml example (RC-E2E-03)
service_code: RC-E2E-03
service_version: 1.0.0
mcp_host: app-rc-e2e-03-mcp-lab01.azurecontainerapps.io
identity: mi-apex-merch-mcp
tools:

  - name: rc_e2e_03.get_excursion_decision_panel
    pattern: read
    gold_mart: g_excursion_decision_panel
    description: |
      Fetch the per-event decision panel for a cold-chain excursion: SKU lot,
      current inventory position, downstream risk, recent markdown history.
    consumed_by_agents: [the-analyst, the-finance-lead, the-operations-lead]

  - name: rc_e2e_03.get_markdown_proposal_basis
    pattern: read
    gold_mart: g_markdown_proposal_basis
    description: Per SKU-store, return elasticity, competitor price, current markdown, demand forecast.
    consumed_by_agents: [the-demand-checker, the-finance-lead]

  - name: rc_e2e_03.commit_markdown_decision
    pattern: decision_emit
    decision_table: MERML.Markdown
    description: Write an approved markdown to MERML and emit LEDGER.
    consumed_by_agents: [the-operations-lead]
    requires_hitl: true

  - name: rc_e2e_03.stage_markdown_approval
    pattern: hitl_stage
    description: Stage a markdown decision for HITL approval via Teams adaptive card.
    consumed_by_agents: [the-operations-lead]
    teams_target: contoso-merchandising-channel

Appendix B is the full MCP tool catalog rolling all 38 Services' manifests into one cross-reference.

Key Takeaways

  • Three MCP tool patterns — read (~70%), decision-emit (~20%), HITL-stage (~10%) — cover every agent interaction in the catalog.
  • The MCP host is a FastAPI app that registers tools at startup, queries Microsoft Fabric Warehouse under mi-apex-merch-mcp, and emits LEDGER rows on every call.
  • mcp-manifest.yaml per Service feeds the host, Microsoft Purview, the orchestrator, and agent prompts; one manifest, four consumers.
Part V

Orchestrations

Bronze, Silver, and Gold are the data plane. Agents and MCP are the reasoning plane. Orchestrations are what turn the two into a Service — workflows that fire on triggers, call agents in sequence, gather HITL approvals, write decisions, and close the audit loop. This part walks the workflow design patterns that recur across all 38 Services, the n8n implementation on the laptop, the Azure Logic Apps Hybrid implementation on Microsoft platform (held in parity by the workflow-intent contract from the Deployment Guide), and the cross-Service composition pattern that lets Services compose at the orchestration layer without coupling at the data layer.

Chapters in this Part

  • Chapter 14Workflow Design Patterns
  • Chapter 15n8n on Laptop
  • Chapter 16Azure Logic Apps Hybrid
  • Chapter 17Cross-Service Composition
Chapter 14

Workflow Design Patterns

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Catalogues the four workflow patterns that recur across the 38-Service catalog.
  • Maps each pattern to the agent composition and HITL gate it implements.
  • Establishes the workflow primitives (trigger · branch · parallel · HITL stage · LEDGER emit) that every Service uses.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Four workflow patterns — triage-and-decide (~18) · continuous-optimization (~8) · detect-and-attribute (~6) · periodic-digest (every Service) — cover the catalog.
  • Seven primitives — trigger · agent call · MCP call · branch · parallel · HITL stage · LEDGER emit — render identically in n8n and Logic Apps Hybrid.
  • The triage-and-decide pattern is the canonical W2-pilot shape from the APEX scenario chains.

14.1 The Four Workflow Patterns

Across all 38 Services, four workflow shapes account for nearly every orchestration. Knowing the pattern names is shorthand for service architects: when an architect says "this Service is a triage-and-decide," every other architect knows roughly what the workflow looks like before reading the JSON.

14.1.1 Triage-and-decide

An event fires; the Analyst severity-ranks it; the Demand Checker quantifies impact; the Finance Lead checks commercial viability; the Operations Lead stages a HITL approval; the human approves or declines; the decision lands in the canonical decision table.

Used by — RC-E2E-03 cold-chain · HLS-E2E-04 prior auth · ER-PU-04 distribution outage · TMT-TEL-NET-02 fault prediction. Approximately 18 of the 38 Services follow this shape.

14.1.2 Continuous-optimization

A scheduled trigger fires; the Demand Checker proposes optimization candidates from a Gold mart; the Finance Lead scores them; the Operations Lead stages a batch HITL approval; approved actions land in canonical decision tables.

Used by — RC-E2E-03 markdown · TH-HOT-03 pricing · TMT-MED-01 content recommendation. Approximately 8 Services.

14.1.3 Detect-and-attribute

An event fires; the Analyst classifies it; an attribution-mart join determines whether to escalate; if so, a HITL stage; the closing decision lands in an audit-attributed table.

Used by — RC-E2E-07 returns fraud · ICE-Aftermarket-03 warranty fraud · TMT-MED-03 ad fraud · TMT-TEL-RAA-04 revenue assurance. Approximately 6 Services.

14.1.4 Periodic-digest

A scheduled trigger fires; multiple Gold marts are read; the Briefer composes a digest; the digest publishes to operator-facing surfaces (Power BI, Teams). No agent decision; no HITL gate; this is the "what happened" loop.

Used by — every Service has at least one periodic-digest workflow. The Briefer is the most-reused agent across the catalog (~32 Services compose it).

14.2 The Workflow Primitives

Every workflow, regardless of pattern, is built from a small set of primitives:

PrimitiveWhat it doesn8n nodeLogic Apps action
TriggerStarts the workflow on an event or scheduleWebhook / ScheduleHTTP Request / Recurrence
Agent callHTTP POST to an agent's /runHTTP RequestHTTP action
MCP callHTTP POST to an MCP toolHTTP RequestHTTP action
BranchConditional routing on a previous step's outputIF / SwitchCondition / Switch
ParallelFan-out to multiple steps in parallel; converge on completionSplit In BatchesParallel branch
HITL stagePause workflow; wait for human approval webhookWait + Webhook resumeApproval connector / custom HTTP wait
LEDGER emitWrite an audit row at a workflow checkpointHTTP Request → harness ledger endpointHTTP action → ledger endpoint

Appendix D documents each primitive at the level of input/output schema and shows how each renders in n8n JSON vs Logic Apps JSON. The Deployment Guide's §2.6.3 establishes the workflow-parity contract; this part lives that contract out at the Service level.

14.3 The Triage-and-Decide Worked Example (RC-E2E-03)

The cold-chain excursion workflow is the canonical triage-and-decide. The shape, in primitives:

  1. Trigger — Eventstream rule (§5.3) emits an excursion event; orchestrator subscribes.
  2. LEDGER emit — workflow start row.
  3. Agent call — POST to The Analyst's /run with excursion payload.
  4. Branch — on Analyst severity score: high → continue, low → close + LEDGER emit.
  5. Parallel — fan out to Demand Checker (forward-demand impact) and Finance Lead (margin-at-risk).
  6. Agent call — Operations Lead synthesizes proposed action (sell-through · markdown · destroy).
  7. HITL stage — adaptive card to the Store Operations Lead via Microsoft Teams.
  8. Branch — on approval: emit decision via rc_e2e_03.commit_markdown_decision; on decline: LEDGER row only.
  9. LEDGER emit — workflow complete row, with hash chain anchor.

Every Service profile in Part VI lists its workflow as primitives. The workflow-intent.md file in the delivery repo is the source; n8n JSON and Logic Apps JSON are derived.

Key Takeaways

  • Four workflow patterns — triage-and-decide, continuous-optimization, detect-and-attribute, periodic-digest — cover every Service.
  • Seven primitives — trigger, agent call, MCP call, branch, parallel, HITL stage, LEDGER emit — render identically in n8n and Logic Apps Hybrid.
  • The triage-and-decide pattern (~18 of 38 Services) is the canonical W2-pilot shape from the APEX scenario chains.
Chapter 14.5

Agent Orchestration Archetypes — Deep Dive

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Distinguishes agent orchestration (how agents coordinate inside a scenario chain) from workflow orchestration (the Chapter 14 patterns + Chapter 15 n8n / Logic Apps Hybrid runtimes).
  • Documents Microsoft's 5 canonical agent-orchestration patterns from Microsoft Agent Framework (the GA successor to Semantic Kernel + AutoGen).
  • Resolves: "Is Semantic Kernel enough? Is n8n enough?" — definitively, against current Microsoft Learn citations.
  • Shows which APEX-M services use which archetype, with the RC-E2E-03 cold-chain Magentic example.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Three layers of orchestration sit on top of each other in every APEX-M deployment, and they answer different questions.
  • Microsoft Agent Framework is the canonical agent orchestrator — it supersedes Semantic Kernel and AutoGen as of 2025/2026 GA. Five built-in patterns: Sequential · Concurrent · Handoff · Group Chat · Magentic.
  • Same Agent Framework code runs on laptop AND on Foundry hosted agents — substrate-awareness is env-var-driven, not code-driven.
  • n8n on laptop = Power Automate / Logic Apps / Foundry workflows on cloud — both are workflow orchestrators (triggers, branches, HITL routing, non-agent workflow steps), not agent orchestrators.
  • Semantic Kernel is now legacy. Existing SK code stays valid; new code uses Agent Framework. Migration guide cited below.

14.5.1 Three Layers of Orchestration

"Orchestration" in APEX is overloaded. Three things coordinate at three different levels, and each runs on its own substrate. Confusing them is the most common architectural mistake.

LayerWhat it answersVocabularyRuns on (laptop / cloud)
1. Scenario workflow pattern What's the overall shape of this scenario's flow? Triage-and-decide · Continuous-optimization · Detect-and-attribute · Periodic-digest (per §14.1) n8n / Logic Apps Hybrid / Foundry workflows
2. Agent orchestration archetype Inside the scenario, how do the agents coordinate? Sequential · Concurrent · Handoff · Group Chat · Magentic (Microsoft Agent Framework) Microsoft Agent Framework — same code on laptop AND Foundry hosted agents
3. Workflow runtime substrate What executes the non-agent workflow steps? Triggers, branches, HITL routing, ticket creation, schedule n8n on laptop · Power Automate / Logic Apps / Foundry workflows on cloud

Layers 1 and 3 are the same answer in different vocabulary — the *workflow* concern. Layer 2 is a different concern entirely: how the agent fleet itself coordinates *inside* a scenario step. Microsoft documents Layer 2 as "agent orchestration patterns" and provides 5 built-in implementations in Microsoft Agent Framework Workflows Orchestrations.

14.5.2 The 5 Microsoft Agent Framework Canonical Patterns

Per AI agent orchestration patterns on Microsoft Learn, the canonical Agent Framework patterns are:

PatternTopologyWhen to useHITL friendly?
SequentialLinear: A → B → CDependent tasks, document-approval-style flows✓ (per Microsoft Learn HITL tutorial)
ConcurrentFan-out + aggregateIndependent operations, ensemble reasoning, parallel fact-gathering✓ (one HITL gate after aggregation)
HandoffMesh: agents transfer control directlyCustomer support, expert systems, dynamic delegation✓ (per-handoff approval)
Group ChatShared conversation, all agents see all messagesCollaborative problem-solving, brainstorming✓ (HITL as a participant)
MagenticManager agent dynamically coordinates specialistsOpen-ended tasks, plans not known up front✓ (manager surfaces HITL gate)
Note — the previous APEX 47-archetype catalog

APEX's Roadmap.md BL.P.65 references "47 orchestration archetypes." Those entries pre-date Microsoft Agent Framework GA. Most of the 47 are *variants* (different agent counts, different HITL placement) of the 5 canonical Microsoft patterns above. The ongoing reconciliation maps each of the 47 to its canonical Microsoft pattern; future amendments to BL.P.65 collapse the 47 into the 5 with parameterization. See ADR-006 for the decision record.

14.5.3 Microsoft Agent Framework — Position vs Semantic Kernel and AutoGen

Per the Agent Framework overview:

Agent Framework combines AutoGen's simple agent abstractions with Semantic Kernel's enterprise features — session-based state management, type safety, middleware, telemetry — and adds graph-based workflows for explicit multi-agent orchestration. The Agent Framework is the direct successor, created by the same teams.

QuestionAnswer
Is Semantic Kernel enough?Existing SK code stays valid. New code uses Microsoft Agent Framework — it's the same teams' next generation, with explicit multi-agent workflow support SK does not have. See Semantic Kernel → Agent Framework migration guide.
Is AutoGen enough?Same answer — Agent Framework is the AutoGen successor too. AutoGen → Agent Framework migration guide.
Is Agent Framework production-ready?Yes. It's the framework Microsoft positions for hosted agents on Foundry Agent Service. See Foundry Hosted Agents.
Can the same Agent Framework code run on laptop?Yes — Agent Framework is open-source and runs anywhere Python or .NET runs. The only thing that changes between substrates is which APEX-Core protocol implementations get injected (mocks on laptop, real Microsoft SDKs on Foundry). See Deploy UX and Substrates.

14.5.4 n8n's Role — Workflow, Not Agent Orchestration

The most common confusion. n8n is documented in Deployment Guide §2.6 and Chapter 15 (this book) as the laptop-substrate workflow runtime. Its role on laptop is identical to Power Automate / Logic Apps Hybrid / Foundry workflows on cloud:

  • Run a trigger when an event arrives
  • Route the event through a branch / parallel / HITL stage / LEDGER emit (the seven §14.2 primitives)
  • Make agent calls — but the orchestration of how agents coordinate inside that call is Microsoft Agent Framework's responsibility, not n8n's

n8n is intentionally not used for agent-to-agent coordination. When the cold-chain scenario has The Pricer reading Demand Checker's output and Finance Lead's elasticity opinion in parallel, that coordination happens inside Microsoft Agent Framework's Magentic workflow — not in an n8n graph. n8n's node for the agent call simply POSTs to the agent fleet endpoint and waits for the workflow's response.

Anti-pattern — orchestrating agents in n8n

It is technically possible to write per-agent n8n nodes and chain them in n8n. Do not do this. n8n graphs are not type-safe, lack agent-runtime state management, and can't model the 5 canonical patterns (especially Magentic and Group Chat) cleanly. Agent orchestration belongs in Microsoft Agent Framework code that the n8n agent-call node invokes. See ADR-006.

14.5.5 Foundry Workflows — The Cloud Substrate Equivalent

On the cloud substrate, APEX-M can use either Microsoft Agent Framework code OR Foundry Agent Service Workflows — declarative YAML or visual designer in the Foundry portal — to coordinate agents. The choice:

Microsoft Agent Framework (code)Foundry Workflows (visual / YAML)
DeterminismFull deterministic control"Primarily nondeterministic" per Microsoft Learn
CustomizationCode path arbitraryBranching + HITL + sequential / group chat
MaintenanceEngineering team ownsLower-skill operators can edit visually
APEX-M defaultYes (via Foundry Hosted Agents)Optional: use when scenario benefits from visual editing

For RC-E2E-03 (deterministic, audit-required, KPI-attributed), Microsoft Agent Framework is the right choice — APEX uses Foundry's hosted-agent hosting model with Agent Framework code inside. Foundry workflows are reserved for use cases where engagement teams need to edit the orchestration without re-deploying agent code.

14.5.6 Worked Example — RC-E2E-03 Cold-Chain Excursion

Mapping the canonical Services Guide §18.1 example through the three layers:

RC-E2E-03 cold-chain excursion · three orchestration layers
Layer 1: scenario workflow pattern
  → Triage-and-decide (per §14.1.1)
  Eventstream Activator fires excursion event → orchestrator dispatches
  agent fleet → HITL gate at decide step → audit row → KPI rollup

Layer 2: agent orchestration archetype (Microsoft Agent Framework)
  → Magentic
  The Analyst is the manager agent. It dynamically dispatches:
    - The Demand Checker  (forward-demand impact)
    - The Pricer          (per-SKU markdown proposal with sim P&L)
    - The Finance Lead    (margin-at-risk scoring)
    - The Operations Lead (HITL Adaptive Card synthesis)
    - The Briefer         (audit-row narrative)
  Order is not predetermined; The Analyst decides who runs based on
  the excursion's classification. HITL gate fires when proposed
  markdown > 30% (per use-case hitl_thresholds).

Layer 3: workflow runtime substrate
  → laptop:  n8n         (per Chapter 15)
  → cloud:   Eventstream + Activator destination + Foundry workflow
              (per Deployment Guide §2.6.2)
  The same workflow primitives (§14.2) render in both — different
  JSON dialect, identical contract.

The three layers are explicit in the use case YAML — see the orchestration_archetype field in services/_use-case.schema.md and the populated example at services/rc/RC-E2E-03/use-cases/_default/use-case.yaml.

14.5.7 Per-RC-Service Archetype Assignment

First-iteration RC-only assignment, validated against the Services Guide §18 service profiles:

ServiceScenario workflow (§14.1)Agent orchestration (§14.5.2)Why
RC-E2E-03 (Pricing)Triage-and-decideMagenticThe Analyst dynamically picks specialists based on excursion class
RC-E2E-04 (Loyalty)Continuous-optimizationSequentialScore → offer-design → finance → ops · linear approval chain
RC-E2E-05 (OSA)Triage-and-decideSequentialGap-detect → priority-score → task-dispatch · simple linear
RC-E2E-07 (Returns Fraud)Detect-and-attributeConcurrent + HITLPattern-match across customer/payment/device graphs in parallel
RC-E2E-09 (Product Tracking)Detect-and-attribute + Periodic-digestHandoffCompliance officer hands off to specialist sub-agents per FSMA-204 lot class
Best Practice — pick archetype during use-case design

The use-case YAML's orchestration_archetype field must be set when authoring a new use case. The wizard validates it's one of the five canonical patterns. Engagements that need a sixth pattern raise an ADR (per adr/) — extending the archetype list is a framework decision, not a per-engagement decision.

14.5.8 Cross-References

  • Microsoft Agent Framework Workflow Orchestrations — canonical pattern docs
  • AI agent orchestration patterns — Azure Architecture Center
  • Semantic Kernel → Agent Framework migration guide
  • AutoGen → Agent Framework migration guide
  • Foundry workflows — visual / YAML alternative
  • Copilot Studio multi-agent patterns — for low-code engagements
  • ADR-006 — Microsoft Agent Framework as canonical agent orchestrator

Key Takeaways

  • Three layers of orchestration: scenario workflow pattern (§14) · agent orchestration archetype (this chapter) · workflow runtime substrate (§15 / Foundry workflows / Logic Apps Hybrid).
  • Microsoft Agent Framework is the canonical agent orchestrator — successor to Semantic Kernel and AutoGen. Five built-in patterns: Sequential, Concurrent, Handoff, Group Chat, Magentic.
  • Same Agent Framework code runs on laptop (Docker Desktop) and Foundry hosted agents (Azure prod) — substrate-awareness is env-var-driven.
  • n8n is the laptop workflow runtime, not an agent orchestrator. n8n calls into Agent Framework code; it does not replace it.
  • Semantic Kernel is now legacy; existing code stays valid, new code uses Agent Framework.
  • Per-service archetype assignment lives in the use case YAML's orchestration_archetype field.
Chapter 15

n8n on Laptop

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the n8n container in docker-compose and how it reaches agents and the harness.
  • Maps each workflow primitive (§14.2) to the specific n8n node that implements it on the laptop.
  • Establishes the file layout for n8n workflows in the delivery repo.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • n8n runs as a docker-compose container with persistent Postgres-backed workflow storage; reaches agents and harness over the compose network.
  • Each of the seven workflow primitives maps to a specific n8n node; the mapping is uniform across all Services.
  • workflow.intent.md + workflow.n8n.json + workflow.logicapp.json live together; CI parity gate enforces sync.
  • Laptop HITL stub mirrors Teams adaptive-card call shape — same orchestration logic, different surface.

15.1 The Compose Setup

n8n runs as a single container alongside Postgres, the test harness, and the agent containers. It is configured with a persistent volume for workflow JSON and credentials, exposes the n8n editor UI on localhost:5678, and uses the same compose network the agents are on so HTTP calls to agent /run endpoints work over service names.

YAML — docker-compose.yml (n8n excerpt)
services:
  n8n:
    image: n8nio/n8n:1.62.0
    container_name: apex-n8n
    ports:
      - "5678:5678"
    environment:
      N8N_HOST: localhost
      N8N_PORT: 5678
      N8N_PROTOCOL: http
      N8N_BASIC_AUTH_ACTIVE: "true"
      N8N_BASIC_AUTH_USER: developer
      N8N_BASIC_AUTH_PASSWORD: devonly
      DB_TYPE: postgresdb
      DB_POSTGRESDB_HOST: postgres
      DB_POSTGRESDB_DATABASE: n8n
      DB_POSTGRESDB_USER: n8n
      DB_POSTGRESDB_PASSWORD: n8n
      GENERIC_TIMEZONE: America/Chicago
      WEBHOOK_URL: http://n8n:5678/
    volumes:
      - n8n-data:/home/node/.n8n
      - ./agentic-merch/services/rc-e2e-03/workflows:/workflows:ro
    depends_on:
      - postgres
    networks:
      - apex-mvp

volumes:
  n8n-data:

15.2 Mapping Primitives to n8n Nodes

Primitiven8n nodeConfiguration notes
Trigger (event)WebhookPath matches the Eventstream-equivalent test fixture; harness POSTs to it
Trigger (schedule)Schedule TriggerCron expression; mirrors Logic Apps Recurrence
Agent callHTTP RequestURL: http://agent-the-analyst:8000/run · POST · JSON body
MCP callHTTP RequestURL: http://mcp-rc-e2e-03:8080/tools/<tool_name>
BranchIF / SwitchCondition expression on previous node's output
ParallelMultiple branches from a node, converged via MergeWait mode = "all"
HITL stageWait → Webhook resumeWebhook URL is sent to a stub HITL UI; resume on POST
LEDGER emitHTTP RequestPOST to harness ledger endpoint http://harness:8080/ledger

15.3 The Workflow File Layout

n8n workflows live in the delivery repo alongside the Logic Apps version, anchored on the shared workflow.intent.md:

FILE LAYOUT — per-Service workflows directory
MVP/agentic-merch/services/rc-e2e-03/workflows/
  excursion-triage/
    workflow.intent.md       # plain-English description (source of truth)
    workflow.n8n.json        # n8n export — laptop substrate
    workflow.logicapp.json   # Logic Apps Standard workflow definition
    fixtures/
      excursion-fired-001.json   # test trigger fixtures the harness drives
      excursion-fired-002.json
  markdown-optimization/
    workflow.intent.md
    workflow.n8n.json
    workflow.logicapp.json
    fixtures/
      markdown-batch-2026-05-09.json

The CI parity gate (Deployment Guide §2.6.3) verifies that the n8n.json and logicapp.json implement the same intent — same step names, same agent calls, same branch conditions. Drift fails the build before either substrate sees it.

15.4 Stubbing the HITL Loop on Laptop

The HITL stage primitive on Azure resolves to a Microsoft Teams adaptive card. On the laptop, there is no Teams. The MVP ships a tiny HITL stub — a FastAPI app on http://hitl-stub:8090 that renders a minimal HTML form with "Approve / Decline" buttons. n8n's Wait node holds the workflow until the form POSTs back. From the workflow's perspective, the call shape is identical to what Teams will produce in production.

Key Takeaways

  • n8n runs as a docker-compose container with persistent Postgres-backed workflow storage; reaches agents and harness over the compose network.
  • Each of the seven workflow primitives maps to a specific n8n node; the mapping is uniform across all Services.
  • workflow.intent.md + workflow.n8n.json + workflow.logicapp.json live together in the per-Service workflows directory; CI parity gate enforces sync.
  • HITL stub on laptop renders a minimal form; the call shape matches Teams adaptive cards on Azure.
Chapter 16

Azure Logic Apps Hybrid

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Walks the Logic Apps Hybrid runtime hosted on Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments.
  • Maps each workflow primitive to its Azure Logic Apps action and shows the Bicep wiring.
  • Connects orchestrator runs to Microsoft Sentinel correlation and Microsoft Cost Management attribution.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Logic Apps Hybrid runs the Logic Apps Standard runtime on the existing Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments — Layer 1 amortized.
  • workflow.logicapp.json is the production rendering of workflow.intent.md; primitive-by-primitive parity with the n8n version.
  • Microsoft Sentinel correlates orchestrator runs with agent traces and LEDGER rows; Microsoft Cost Management attributes orchestrator cost to apex:service-code.

16.1 Why Logic Apps Hybrid (Recap)

The Deployment Guide §2.6.2 establishes Logic Apps Hybrid as the default Azure orchestrator: it runs the Logic Apps Standard runtime on the same Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments environment as the agents, amortizes onto the Layer 1 substrate, authenticates as mi-apex-merch-runtime via Microsoft Entra ID, and resolves secrets natively from Microsoft.KeyVault. From a Service perspective, Logic Apps Hybrid is what runs the workflow.logicapp.json file in production.

16.2 The Logic Apps Workflow Schema

An Azure Logic Apps workflow is a JSON document with two top-level sections — triggers and actions — plus optional outputs and parameter bindings. Each action references a connector or built-in by name and takes typed inputs.

JSON — workflow.logicapp.json (RC-E2E-03 excursion-triage excerpt)
{
  "definition": {
    "$schema": "https://schema.management.azure.com/providers/Microsoft.Logic/schemas/2016-06-01/workflowdefinition.json#",
    "contentVersion": "1.0.0.0",
    "parameters": {
      "agent_analyst_url":  { "type": "string" },
      "mcp_host_url":       { "type": "string" },
      "ledger_endpoint":    { "type": "string" }
    },
    "triggers": {
      "excursion_event": {
        "type": "Request",
        "kind": "Http",
        "inputs": {
          "schema": { "$ref": "#/definitions/ExcursionEventPayload" }
        }
      }
    },
    "actions": {
      "ledger_workflow_start": {
        "type": "Http",
        "inputs": {
          "method": "POST",
          "uri":    "@parameters('ledger_endpoint')",
          "body": {
            "event": "workflow.start",
            "service_code": "RC-E2E-03",
            "workflow": "excursion-triage",
            "trigger_payload": "@triggerBody()"
          }
        }
      },
      "call_the_analyst": {
        "type": "Http",
        "runAfter": { "ledger_workflow_start": ["Succeeded"] },
        "inputs": {
          "method": "POST",
          "uri":    "@{parameters('agent_analyst_url')}/run",
          "body": {
            "service_code": "RC-E2E-03",
            "scenario":     "cold-chain-excursion",
            "inputs":       "@triggerBody()"
          }
        }
      },
      "branch_on_severity": {
        "type": "If",
        "runAfter": { "call_the_analyst": ["Succeeded"] },
        "expression": {
          "and": [
            { "greaterOrEquals": [
                "@body('call_the_analyst')?['severity_score']", 0.7 ] }
          ]
        },
        "actions": {
          "fan_out": { "type": "Scope", "actions": {
            "call_the_demand_checker": { "type": "Http", "inputs": { /* ... */ } },
            "call_the_finance_lead":    { "type": "Http", "inputs": { /* ... */ } }
          }},
          "stage_hitl_approval": {
            "type": "Http",
            "runAfter": { "fan_out": ["Succeeded"] },
            "inputs": {
              "method": "POST",
              "uri":    "@{parameters('mcp_host_url')}/tools/rc_e2e_03.stage_markdown_approval",
              "body":   { /* ... */ }
            }
          }
        },
        "else": {
          "actions": {
            "ledger_low_severity_close": { "type": "Http", "inputs": { /* ... */ } }
          }
        }
      },
      "ledger_workflow_complete": {
        "type": "Http",
        "runAfter": { "branch_on_severity": ["Succeeded","Skipped"] },
        "inputs": { /* ledger close row */ }
      }
    }
  }
}

16.3 Bicep Wiring per Service

The Service-level Bicep deploys a Logic Apps Hybrid Container App per workflow (or per Service workflow set, mounted via a Microsoft.Storage account holding all the JSONs). The Deployment Guide §2.6.2 has the full Bicep; the Service profile in Part VI lists the workflow names and the parameter values flowed in.

16.4 Microsoft Sentinel Correlation and Cost Attribution

Each Logic Apps run emits a run-history event to the Layer 1 Microsoft.OperationalInsights workspace. Microsoft Sentinel rules can correlate orchestrator runs with agent traces and LEDGER rows; for example, a rule that fires when "an HITL approval was staged but no resolved row appeared within 4 hours" gets a correlated view of (workflow run, staged tool call, missing decision row) without join logic.

Microsoft Cost Management attributes the Logic Apps Hybrid Container App's cost to the per-Service tag (apex:service-code). This is what makes the W3 outcome-based commercial envelope feasible — the Service's cost-to-serve is a real number, per Service, per tenant, per month.

Key Takeaways

  • Logic Apps Hybrid runs the Logic Apps Standard runtime on the same Microsoft.App/managedEnvironments environment as agents — Layer 1 amortized.
  • workflow.logicapp.json is the production rendering of workflow.intent.md; primitive-by-primitive parity with workflow.n8n.json.
  • Microsoft Sentinel correlates orchestrator runs with agent traces and LEDGER rows; Microsoft Cost Management attributes orchestrator cost to apex:service-code.
Chapter 17

Cross-Service Composition

What This Chapter Does for You
  • Explains how Services compose at the orchestration layer without coupling at the data layer.
  • Walks the cross-Service MCP-call pattern (e.g., RC-E2E-03 reading SCML.Lot from RC-E2E-09).
  • Establishes the audit-attribution rule that keeps multi-Service decisions cleanly attributable.
TL;DR · 60 seconds
  • Services compose at the MCP layer, never at the data layer; data owners expose tools, consumers call tools.
  • Authorization runs outward from data owners — the data-owner Service's Bicep grants role assignments to the consumer's identity.
  • Cross-Service decisions are attributed to one Service by decision.service_code; supporting tool calls (with row hashes) appear in the evidence array.

17.1 Why Compose at All

The 38-Service catalog is not 38 isolated Services. The cold-chain excursion scenario in Appendix E references both RC-E2E-03 (Pricing & Revenue) and RC-E2E-09 (Product Tracking). The HEDIS care-gap scenario in HLS spans HLS-E2E-06 and HLS-E2E-04. Real-world client problems pull on more than one Service envelope; APEX has to compose at runtime.

17.2 The Composition Rule

Two rules govern cross-Service composition:

  1. Compose at the MCP layer, not the data layer. RC-E2E-03's agent does not read RC-E2E-09's Silver tables directly. Instead, RC-E2E-09 exposes an MCP tool (e.g., rc_e2e_09.get_lot_provenance) that returns the Gold-shaped lot information. RC-E2E-03's agent calls that tool through the MCP host. The audit chain records the cross-Service call as a tool name, not a free-form data join.
  2. Authorize from the data-owner side. The Microsoft.Authorization role assignment that lets RC-E2E-03's mi-apex-merch-mcp identity call rc_e2e_09.get_lot_provenance lives in RC-E2E-09's Bicep. The data owner grants access; the consumer doesn't grant itself access. This makes the access graph readable by walking outward from each data owner.

Both rules trace back to the Deployment Guide's §9.4 cross-Service authorization pattern. The Services Guide is where the rules become operational.

17.3 The Audit-Attribution Rule

When Service A (RC-E2E-03) makes a decision that consumed a fact from Service B (RC-E2E-09), the LEDGER row records:

  • decision.service_code = "RC-E2E-03" — the decision belongs to RC-E2E-03.
  • decision.evidence = [{tool: "rc_e2e_03.get_excursion_decision_panel", row_hash: "..."}, {tool: "rc_e2e_09.get_lot_provenance", row_hash: "..."}] — every supporting MCP call, with row hash.

RC-E2E-03 owns the decision and the KPI attribution; RC-E2E-09 contributed evidence. Both Services' analytics see the relationship — RC-E2E-03 reports its decision count; RC-E2E-09 reports its tool-call count and can detect when its provenance is materially driving a downstream Service's outcome.

17.4 Worked Example — Cold-Chain Excursion (RC-E2E-03 ↔ RC-E2E-09)

  1. Excursion event fires (RC-E2E-03 trigger).
  2. RC-E2E-03 orchestrator calls rc_e2e_03.get_excursion_decision_panel (own MCP host).
  3. The panel includes a lot_id reference but does not include the FSMA-204 chain-of-custody.
  4. RC-E2E-03's Analyst, deciding whether the excursion forces destroy vs sell-through, calls rc_e2e_09.get_lot_provenance(lot_id) via cross-Service MCP routing.
  5. RC-E2E-09's MCP host (separate Container App, separate scoping) returns the Gold-shaped lot provenance with row hash.
  6. RC-E2E-03's Analyst incorporates the provenance into its severity score; the LEDGER row for the eventual decision references both tool calls.

Cross-Service composition does not change the workflow shape; it adds an MCP call. From the orchestrator's point of view, calling RC-E2E-09's MCP tool is identical to calling RC-E2E-03's — same primitive, same audit row. The Independence boundary, the Service-scoped identity, and the row-hash-on-LEDGER property all hold.

Key Takeaways

  • Services compose at the MCP layer, never at the data layer; data owners expose tools, consumers call tools.
  • The data-owner Service's Bicep grants role assignments to the consumer Service's identity — access graph is readable outward from data owners.
  • Cross-Service decisions are attributed to one Service by decision.service_code; supporting tool calls (with row hashes) appear in the evidence array.
  • Workflow shape is unchanged; an extra MCP call is the only addition. Same primitive, same audit row.

18.1 RC-E2E-03 — Pricing & Revenue Decision Service (Flagship)

RC-E2E-03 · ⭐ FLAGSHIP

Pricing & Revenue Decision Service — Cold-Chain & Markdown

Domain: Pricing & Revenue · Practice: Retail & Consumer

KPI Envelope

Gross Margin +3.2pp · Days-on-Hand −28% · Markdown-to-Clear +41% · per-event margin protected ~$142K (cold-chain) · decision-loop 5.7 hr → <90 sec

Scenarios in Scope (W2 Pilot)

  • Cold-chain excursion mid-shift — sell-through vs markdown vs destroy decision
  • Dynamic markdown optimization — weekly per-SKU-store markdown cadence

Data Sources (Bronze)

SORVendor patternBronze landingCadence
POSNCR Aloha · Toshiba · Oracle Retail POSbr_pos_transactions (KQL DB · Eventstream)Streaming
ERPSAP S/4HANA Retail · Oracle Retail · Manhattan WMSbr_erp_inventory (Lakehouse · Mirroring)Near-real-time CDC
Refrigeration telemetryWalk-in monitor + per-zone sensors · client IoT Hubbr_refrigeration_telemetry (KQL DB · Eventstream)Streaming
Competitor pricingNielsen · Numerator · web-scraping feedbr_competitor_pricing (Lakehouse · Pipeline)Daily 05:00

Silver Schemas

  • SCML.Inventory — conformed inventory positions per SKU-store-zone with on_hand, on_order, reserved, available
  • SCML.Lot — FSMA-204 lot provenance, chain-of-custody, cold-chain history (read via cross-Service from RC-E2E-09)
  • PROML.Pricing — base-price register per SKU-store with effective_from and currency stabilization (read by The Pricing Agent)
  • PROML.DiscountRule — category- and store-tier-scoped discount rules with approval-path metadata
  • MERML.Markdown — historical markdowns with reason_code and decision_ledger_id
  • MERML.Elasticity — SKU-store demand elasticity coefficients (90/180-day estimation windows)
  • MERML.Competitor — competitor price observations conformed to SKU-equivalent

Gold Marts

  • g_excursion_decision_panel · decision-panel · per-event mart for The Analyst (§12.2.1)
  • g_markdown_proposal_basis · decision-panel · per-SKU-store mart for Demand Checker + Finance Lead
  • g_pricing_recommendation_basis · decision-panel · per-SKU-store mart joining elasticity, competitor, current price register, recent markdown history, and category margin floors — backs The Pricing Agent
  • g_inventory_position_current · point-in-time · current-state mart
  • g_kpi_rc_e2e_03_daily · time-series · KPI rollup mart (§12.2.2)
  • g_markdown_outcome_attribution · attribution · realized vs baseline gross-margin lift

Agents Composed

  • The Analyst — severity-rank excursion events; recommend action class
  • The Demand Checker — quantify forward-demand impact under proposed action
  • The Pricing Agent — compute the recommended price/markdown given elasticity + competitor + business-rule constraints; emit a price proposal with a confidence interval. Sits between the Demand Checker (forecast) and the Finance Lead (margin scoring) in the workflow. Reads PROML.Pricing, PROML.DiscountRule, and MERML.Elasticity via the g_pricing_recommendation_basis Gold mart. The Pricing Agent is also the headline beneficiary of the LEDGER + Redis Cache learning loop in §25.8 — a Year-1 rules-driven agent that becomes a Year-3 superagent through accumulated decisions and operator-preference memory.
  • The Finance Lead — score margin-at-risk and commercial viability of the Pricing Agent's recommendation
  • The Operations Lead — synthesize HITL adaptive card; route to Store Operations
  • The Briefer — compose end-of-shift digest of decisions and outcomes

MCP Tools

  • rc_e2e_03.get_excursion_decision_panel · read · Analyst, Finance, Ops
  • rc_e2e_03.get_markdown_proposal_basis · read · Demand Checker, Finance
  • rc_e2e_03.get_pricing_recommendation_basis · read · The Pricing Agent (elasticity · competitor · price register · rule library · operator preference snapshot from Redis)
  • rc_e2e_03.get_similar_pricing_decisions · read · The Pricing Agent (Redis-backed episodic memory · last-N approved markdowns by category-store-season)
  • rc_e2e_03.commit_markdown_decision · decision-emit · Operations Lead
  • rc_e2e_03.stage_markdown_approval · HITL-stage · Operations Lead
  • Cross-Service · rc_e2e_09.get_lot_provenance (data owned by RC-E2E-09)

Workflow Shape

Triage-and-decide. See §14.3 for the worked example primitive sequence. With The Pricing Agent added, the parallel fan-out after The Analyst now branches three ways — Demand Checker (forecast) · The Pricing Agent (price recommendation reading the Demand Checker output as input where serial dependency exists) · Finance Lead (commercial scoring of the Pricing Agent's proposal) — converging into the Operations Lead's HITL stage. Workflow files: excursion-triage/ and markdown-optimization/.

Personas (HITL)

  • Marisol Reyes · Store Operations Lead — approves excursion sell-through/markdown/destroy via Teams adaptive card
  • Daniel Chen · Merchandising Director — reviews weekly markdown proposal in Power BI; approves via Copilot chat

KPI Attribution Path

Decisions land in MERML.Markdown with decision_ledger_id → g_markdown_outcome_attribution joins LEDGER decisions with subsequent realized margin → g_kpi_rc_e2e_03_daily rolls up to GM/DoH/markdown-to-clear → Power BI Direct Lake serves the operator dashboard.

Catalog Drills

  1. HLS-E2E-04 Prior Authorization is the Ch 27 worked example. If not, the configuration is short-circuiting Tier-4 protection.
  2. HLS-E2E-06 HEDIS Care-Gap is value-based-contract driven. Sketch the attribution mart that would prove the +$3.8M VBC bonus claim from the Sellers Guide. Which LEDGER columns and which Silver entities does it join?
  3. HLS-LS-03 Trial Matching depends on EHR + trial-protocol JSON. Identify the Year-1 placeholder eligibility criteria (rule-based) vs the Year-2 LEDGER-trained criteria (Ch 28 §28.4). Which protocols would benefit most from Year-2 training first?

  • OEMML, WLML, SCML, and QMSML are the ICE schema families · WLML cross-references QMSML for warranty-traceback (same pattern as AXLE).
