I.8 How to Use This Index

Three patterns:

  1. Forward lookup — reading a section in Volume X, want the sibling treatment? Find the row, scan the column for Volume Y.
  2. Concept gap-finding — a row with only one or two filled columns flags where a topic is documented in one volume only. Useful when planning a v2 edition: which gaps deserve a sibling-volume treatment?
  3. Maintenance discipline — when adding new content to any volume, update this index. Cross-references that exist in prose but not in this index are the typical drift point during edits.
Appendix H

Operational Runbooks

Ten failure classes, each with a detection rule (typically Microsoft Sentinel KQL) · diagnosis steps (queries the on-call runs first) · recovery procedure (commands and decision points) · prevention (the policy or telemetry change that keeps it from recurring). This appendix is the on-call's 3am companion. Read each section once before you need it.

H.1 Bronze Ingestion Stall

Symptom: A SOR Bronze table stops gaining rows. Streaming Bronze (Eventstream) shows a backlog; batch Bronze (Pipeline) shows the most recent partition lagging.

H.1.1 Detection

KQL — Sentinel rule
// Bronze freshness alert · per Service per source
customEvents
| where name == "bronze.row.landed"
| summarize last_landed = max(timestamp)
    by tostring(customDimensions.service_code), tostring(customDimensions.sor_id)
| extend lag_min = datetime_diff('minute', now(), last_landed)
| where lag_min > 30  // expected cadence × 2
| project service_code, sor_id, last_landed, lag_min

H.1.2 Diagnosis

  1. Eventstream connection state — is the upstream broker (Event Hubs · Kafka · IoT Hub) emitting? If not, the SOR is the issue and ownership passes to client ops.
  2. Tokenization key access — has the mi-apex-merch-ingest identity lost Key Vault Crypto User? Check Microsoft.Authorization role assignments at Key Vault scope.
  3. Microsoft Fabric capacity exhaustion — is the F-SKU at > 95% sustained? If so, see H.7.
  4. Schema mismatch — has the SOR added a non-nullable column the Bronze landing schema doesn't accept? Check Pipeline run logs.

H.1.3 Recovery

  • If client SOR — escalate to client ops; APEX side waits.
  • If tokenization key — restore role assignment via Bicep redeploy of identity.bicep (Deployment Guide Appendix A).
  • If schema mismatch — add a nullable Bronze column (Bronze contract is permissive · §1.3.1); rerun the Pipeline.
  • If capacity — see H.7.

H.1.4 Prevention

  • Bronze freshness telemetry per (service_code, sor_id) — Microsoft Purview lineage already captures source registration; ensure heartbeat events fire.
  • Schema drift detection — Microsoft Purview classification rules emit alerts on new columns appearing in Bronze.

H.2 Silver Build Failure

Symptom: A Silver-build notebook errors out (PySpark exception · T-SQL stored-proc failure · KQL update-policy reject) and the Silver table is empty or stale.

H.2.1 Detection

KQL — Silver build failure rule
FabricNotebookRuns_CL
| where Status_s == "Failed"
| where NotebookName_s startswith "silver-build-"
| project TimeGenerated, NotebookName_s, ErrorMessage_s,
          ServiceCode_s = extract(@"silver-build-([\w-]+)-", 1, NotebookName_s)
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1h)

H.2.2 Diagnosis

  1. Read the error: type mismatch · null violation · join failure on id_map · Bronze partition missing.
  2. If type mismatch — Bronze landed a column with an unexpected type (SOR change). Check the src_* column for what the SOR sent.
  3. If join failure on id_map — a new SOR-internal ID arrived without a canonical mapping. The id_map Silver entity needs a row inserted.
  4. If Bronze partition missing — see H.1.

H.2.3 Recovery

  • Type mismatch — fix the build notebook's cast logic; idempotent rebuild from Bronze (§8.2). No data loss; rebuild is byte-identical.
  • id_map missing — insert the canonical mapping row; rerun the build for the affected partition.
  • Resume Gold-build pipelines that depend on the Silver entity (the orchestrator triggers them on Silver completion).

H.2.4 Prevention

The §10.3 quality gate's schema check catches type mismatch at promotion time before Gold rebuilds, limiting the blast radius. Quality-gate failures alert on-call but do not block Bronze ingest — the Silver build holds while you fix.

H.3 Gold Mart Staleness

Symptom: Agents return apparently-fresh data with an old materialized_at. Operators see the Power BI dashboard hasn't moved.

H.3.1 Detection

KQL — Gold staleness
customEvents
| where name == "mcp.tool.called"
| extend materialized_at = todatetime(customDimensions.materialized_at)
| where materialized_at < ago(1h)
| summarize stale_calls = count() by tostring(customDimensions.tool_name)
| where stale_calls > 5

H.3.2 Diagnosis

  1. Is the Gold-rebuild Pipeline running? Microsoft Fabric Pipeline run history.
  2. Is Microsoft Fabric capacity throttling Direct Lake auto-refresh? Check F-SKU utilization.
  3. Did Silver finish? If Silver is still building, Gold-on-event won't fire.

H.3.3 Recovery

  • Manually trigger the Gold-build Pipeline · Microsoft Fabric Data Pipelines UI or CLI.
  • If Direct Lake — reset the semantic model's underlying table reference (Microsoft Fabric Workspace settings).
  • Notify operators via the Briefer's emergency-digest path that the dashboard was stale for X minutes.

H.4 MCP Tool Drift

Symptom: An agent calls an MCP tool and gets a response shape that doesn't match the agent's expected output schema. The runner rejects the response; the agent retries; the LLM cost climbs.

H.4.1 Detection

Microsoft.Insights custom event mcp.tool.schema_mismatch emitted by the runner when output schema validation fails > 3× in a 1-minute window.

H.4.2 Diagnosis

  1. Did a Gold mart change shape (column added · type changed)? Compare the mart's current schema to the MCP tool's output_schema.
  2. Did the MCP host get a new manifest version that diverged from the agent's expectation? Check mcp-manifest.yaml diff history.

H.4.3 Recovery

  • Roll back the MCP host Container App revision (Deployment Guide §15.2 traffic-split snap-back).
  • If the mart change is intentional, bump the consuming agent's image version with the updated output-schema reference (Ch 28 §28.6).

H.4.4 Prevention

MCP tool output_schema is registered in Microsoft Purview as a versioned asset. Any Gold mart shape change that breaks an MCP tool emits a Purview lineage alert before the change reaches production.

H.5 Agent Behavior Regression

Symptom: Container Apps health is green; LLM calls succeed; LEDGER chain verifies. But operator HITL acceptance rate has dropped, or KPI attribution has shifted unfavorably. The platform looks fine; the agent has gotten worse.

H.5.1 Detection

The §28.5.3 live-shadow + canary motion is the primary detection. If a regression escapes canary, post-deployment telemetry catches it via:

  • HITL acceptance-rate drop > 5pp week-over-week.
  • Cost-per-decision rise > 30% without input-volume change (more retries · larger contexts).
  • KPI attribution mart shows realized lift dropping back toward baseline.

H.5.2 Diagnosis & Recovery

  1. Snap rollback to the prior agent revision (Deployment Guide §15 traffic-split → 100% prior).
  2. Run the Ch 28 §28.5.1 golden-set evaluation against the released version. If it fails, the golden set was insufficient — augment with the cases that escaped.
  3. Re-train Year-2 few-shot exemplars from updated LEDGER (Ch 28 §28.4); ship the next image version with the augmented few-shot set.

H.6 LEDGER Chain Break

Symptom: verify_chain returns ok=false. A row's _prev_hash doesn't match the chain. This is a Sev-1. Audit trail is suspect until reconciled.

H.6.1 Detection

KQL — chain-verify failure rule
customEvents
| where name == "ledger.chain.verify"
| where customDimensions.ok != "true"
| summarize broken_at = max(tostring(customDimensions.broken_at_id))
    by tostring(customDimensions.service_code)

H.6.2 Diagnosis

  1. Identify the broken row (broken_at_id from verify result).
  2. Was the LEDGER table modified out-of-band (someone ran a manual UPDATE)? Check Microsoft Defender for Cloud anomalous-write alerts.
  3. Was a row inserted with an explicit _hash/_prev_hash that the runner didn't compute? Test harness misconfiguration.
  4. Is the HSM signing key reachable? Microsoft.KeyVault rate-limit at the checkpoint cadence.

H.6.3 Recovery

  • If out-of-band modification — Sev-1 incident response. Suspend HITL approvals for the Service. File a regulatory note before any rollback. Recovery requires audit reconstruction from Microsoft Fabric Bronze + attribution mart joined with the last-known-good LEDGER state.
  • If harness misconfig — fix the runner; backfill the chain forward from the broken row by re-deriving hashes.
  • If HSM rate-limit — increase checkpoint cadence (sign less frequently · sign batches); ride out the limit window.

H.6.4 Prevention

Microsoft.KeyVault HSM checkpoint is the cryptographic anchor (§25.2). Database append-only enforcement on the LEDGER table — REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE for every identity except a break-glass admin role — is what makes out-of-band modification detectable. Microsoft Sentinel UEBA on the break-glass identity catches the rare actual use.

H.7 Microsoft Fabric Capacity Exhaustion

Symptom: Pipelines queue · notebook runs throttled · Direct Lake serves slow.

H.7.1 Detection

Microsoft Fabric Capacity Metrics app surfaces sustained > 90% utilization. Set Sentinel rule on FabricCapacityMetrics_CL · CapacityUnits / Available_d > 0.9 for 30m.

H.7.2 Recovery

  • Immediate — pause non-critical pipelines (the Briefer's daily-digest can wait an hour; the excursion-decision-panel materialization cannot).
  • Short-term — bump F-SKU one tier (Ch 29 §29.3 sizing matrix).
  • Long-term — Ch 29 §29.6 right-size triggers and §29.7 optimization patterns.

H.8 Microsoft Entra ID Auth Failures

Symptom: Container Apps fail to pull from registry · MCP host can't acquire Key Vault tokens · agents log 401 against MCP.

H.8.1 Diagnosis & Recovery

  1. Conditional Access change — was a tenant-wide policy update applied that affects managed identities? Microsoft Entra ID audit log.
  2. Identity scope rotation — was a managed identity object ID rotated (rare, but happens with re-creation)? Bicep redeploy ensures Container App is bound to the new identity.
  3. Key Vault firewall — did Private Endpoint configuration change? Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints state.

H.9 Microsoft.KeyVault HSM Rate Limit

Symptom: LEDGER checkpoint signing fails intermittently with HTTP 429. Chain verify still passes (uncheckpointed rows still have valid _hash) but the cryptographic checkpoint cadence falls behind target.

Recovery: Increase checkpoint interval (sign every 10K rows or every 5min instead of every 1K rows or every 1min). Ch 25 §25.2 covers the rate-limit calculus. Premium tier supports hundreds of ops/sec but bursts beyond that throttle.

H.10 Cross-Service Tool Authorization Break

Symptom: Service A calls Service B's MCP tool and gets 403 Forbidden. Cross-Service composition (§17.4) breaks.

H.10.1 Diagnosis & Recovery

  1. The data-owner Service's Bicep grants role assignments to the consumer's mi-apex-merch-mcp identity (Deployment Guide §9.4). Check the data-owner's Bicep for missing or rolled-back role assignments.
  2. Microsoft.Authorization propagation can lag 5–10 minutes after Bicep apply. If recently changed, wait and retry.
  3. Bicep redeploy of the data-owner's Service module restores the role assignment.

H.11 Runbook Template (for Service-Specific Additions)

When you encounter a Service-specific failure not on this list, capture it in the same shape and contribute it back to this appendix:

MARKDOWN — runbook template

2. _Second check..._
3. _..._

### Recovery

- _Action 2_

### Prevention
_The telemetry, policy, or architectural change that keeps this from recurring._
